These are diagnostic executions under competing CPU activity, not a clean paired speed comparison. All four finish inside their physical process budgets. They reuse the deployed trunk, quantization-aware expert kernels and bounded prefill sweep, while retaining exact standalone authentication, independently loaded original drafts, explicit resource accounting and a 32K text-only bound. No pack is added to supported selection, download offers or Auto.

The first run retained the reference implementation’s small allocator cache and was slower. The second restores the ordinary deployed allocator allowance. The third additionally reuses the checked small-row projections and split attention during draft verification. The fourth runs that same third recipe at the lower 14 GB ceiling. Each prior variant and receipt is preserved; later observations do not replace an earlier result. A useful latency trend is an engineering lead, not promotion evidence. Native arithmetic differs from the reference path, so its prior parity and quality receipts are not inherited.

user is asking about a technical topic related to local AI\/ML inference, specifically about how a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) model can use SSD streaming when its weights exceed RAM. They also want me to distinguish between a \"saved memory ceiling\" and \"current allocation.\"\n\nLet me think about what this actually means technically:\n\n1. **MoE models**: These have many \"expert\" sub-networks (e.g., Mixtral 8x7B has 8 experts per layer, only 2 are active per token). The total parameter count is large, but only a subset of experts are activated per forward pass.\n\n2. **SSD streaming**: When the total model weights don't fit in RAM, you can keep some weights on SSD (NVMe) and stream them into RAM on demand. For MoE models, this is particularly feasible because only a few experts are active per token, so you can page in the needed expert weights from SSD to RAM, compute, then page out.\n\n3. **Saved user is asking about a technical topic related to local AI\/ML inference, specifically about how a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) model can use SSD streaming when its weights exceed RAM. They also want me to distinguish between a \"saved memory ceiling\" and \"current allocation.\"\n\nLet me think about what this actually means technically:\n\n1. **MoE models**: These have many \"expert\" sub-networks (e.g., Mixtral 8x7B has 8 experts per layer, only 2 are active per token). The total parameter count is large, but only a subset of experts are activated per forward pass.\n\n2. **SSD streaming**: When the total model weights don't fit in RAM, you can keep some weights on SSD (NVMe) and stream them into RAM on demand. For MoE models, this is particularly feasible because only a few experts are active per token, so you can page in the needed expert weights from SSD to RAM, compute, then page out.\n\n3. **Saved user is asking about a technical concept related to running local AI models (mixture-of-experts, or MoE) when model weights exceed available RAM, using \"SSD streaming.\" They also want me to distinguish between a \"saved memory ceiling\" and \"current allocation.\"\n\nLet me think about what this actually means technically:\n\n1. **Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models**: These are models like Mixtral, DeepSeek-V3, etc., where only a subset of \"expert\" networks are activated per token. The total parameter count is huge, but only a fraction is active per forward pass.\n\n2. **SSD streaming when weights exceed RAM**: This refers to techniques like llama.cpp's `--n-gpu-layers` offloading, or more specifically, memory-mapped file loading (mmap) where model weights are stored on SSD and paged into RAM on demand. For MoE models specifically, since only a few experts are active per token, you can keep inactive experts on SSD and only load the active ones into RAM. This is sometimes called \"expert offloading\" or \"layer offloading.\" The OS virtual memory system (page faults) handles bringing weights from SSD into RAM as needed.\n\n3. 0.287694425000005,"visionQueryTile":0,"visionQueryTileCalls":0},"text":"The user is asking about a technical concept related to running local AI models (mixture-of-experts, or MoE) when model weights exceed available RAM, using \"SSD streaming.\" They also want me to distinguish between a \"saved memory ceiling\" and \"current allocation.\"\n\nLet me think about what this actually means technically:\n\n1. **Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models**: These are models like Mixtral, DeepSeek-V3, etc., where only a subset of \"expert\" networks are activated per token. The total parameter count is huge, but only a fraction is active per forward pass.\n\n2. **SSD streaming when weights exceed RAM**: This refers to techniques like llama.cpp's `--n-gpu-layers` offloading, or more specifically, memory-mapped file loading (mmap) where model weights are stored on SSD and paged into RAM on demand. For MoE models specifically, since only a few experts are active per token, you can keep inactive experts on SSD and only load the active ones into RAM.
